Usually I just have to find some excuse to get up and move around. \n\nThat's... pretty much it.\n\nDoctor: Does this restlessness happen only in situations where you're expected to sit still, like meetings, or does it happen at other times too?\n\nPatient: Um... no, it's other times too, I suppose. \n\nWhen I'm on my shift... I just go from mopping to checking the trash, maybe straightening up chairs. I don't really stop. And... even when I get home, I don't usually sit down. I'll just go out to the garage and putter around, or... re-sort my tools. Just keep moving, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel impatient or irritated when you're unable to move around?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I haven't really... paid attention to that. I don't think I get mad or anything. I just... prefer to be doing something, I guess. I haven't really thought about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse, doing things without fully thinking them through?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so. I haven't really noticed anything like that. I just... go about my day, mostly. Do my job. I don't really make a lot of sudden decisions or anything.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I'll just go out to the garage and putter around, or... re-sort my tools. Just keep moving, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel impatient or irritated when you're unable to move around?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I haven't really... paid attention to that. My work is... pretty routine. I just do what I'm supposed to. I haven't really thought about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse, doing things without fully thinking them through?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so. I haven't really noticed anything like that. I just... go about my day, mostly. Do my job. I don't really make a lot of sudden decisions or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention to details or making careless mistakes in your work or daily activities?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My work is... pretty routine. I just do what I'm supposed to. I haven't had anyone tell me I'm missing things or... messing up, so I think I do fine with that.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice that you had a hard time sitting still?\n\nPatient: I guess... it goes back a long way. Even in grade school, I remember getting in trouble for popping out of my seat. I'd talk over the teacher or just rush ahead before they finished the directions. My mother... she used to say I acted like I was wound too tight, even back then. So, I guess it's always been there.\n\nDoctor: Does this restlessness and need to keep moving happen in all parts of your life, like at home, at work, and when you're with friends?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. On my shifts, I just... keep moving. I'll go right from mopping to checking the trash, straightening chairs... I don't really stop. And at home, it's mostly the same. I don't really just sit down after work. I'll go out and start puttering in the garage, maybe... re-sorting my tools or something. Just staying busy, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on a task that you lose track of time or forget about everything else around you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I just... I do my work and move on to the next thing. I don't really get stuck on one thing for hours like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to keep moving and restlessness causes problems for you in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: I guess it does, yeah. At work... well, there have been some complaints lately. I tend to miss parts of what they tell me to do, just rushing through, I suppose. It causes some friction with the others, especially when we're turning over rooms or trying to figure out supplies. It got to the point where my supervisor... he suggested I look into the employee assistance program. So, yeah, it's causing some issues.\n\nDoctor: Does your restlessness or need to keep moving ever cause problems for you in your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I mean... my wife says I can't sit still through a movie, but... I don't know if I'd call it a problem. We just... we don't talk about it much.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your need to keep moving is triggered by feeling anxious, stressed, or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I'm not really... I don't get anxious or stressed like that. I just... I just like to keep busy. It's just how I am, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your restlessness and need to keep moving are causing you a lot of distress or interfering with your ability to get along with others or do your job?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I was saying before, it's mostly just at work. Getting complaints and... having to be corrected because I missed parts of the instructions. It just causes friction with the other cleaners when we're doing the room turnovers or sorting out supplies. My supervisor wouldn't have pushed me toward that assistance program if it wasn't... you know, interfering. So... yeah. I guess it does.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

My mother... she used to say I acted like I was wound too tight, even back then. So, I guess it's always been there.\n\nDoctor: Does this restlessness and need to keep moving happen in all parts of your life, like at home, at work, and when you're with friends?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. On my shifts, I just... keep moving. I'll go right from mopping to checking the trash, straightening chairs... I don't really stop. And at home, it's mostly the same. I don't really just sit down after work. I'll go out and start puttering in the garage, maybe... re-sorting my tools or something. Just staying busy, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on a task that you lose track of time or forget about everything else around you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I just... I do my work and move on to the next thing. I don't really get stuck on one thing for hours like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to keep moving and restlessness causes problems for you in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: I guess it does, yeah. At work... well, there have been some complaints lately. I tend to miss parts of what they tell me to do, just rushing through, I suppose. It causes some friction with the others, especially when we're turning over rooms or trying to figure out supplies. It got to the point where my supervisor... he suggested I look into the employee assistance program. So, yeah, it's causing some issues.\n\nDoctor: Does your restlessness or need to keep moving ever cause problems for you in your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I mean... my wife says I can't sit still through a movie, but... I don't know if I'd call it a problem. We just... we don't talk about it much.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your need to keep moving is triggered by feeling anxious, stressed, or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I'm not really... I don't get anxious or stressed like that. I just... I just like to keep busy. It's just how I am, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your restlessness and need to keep moving are causing you a lot of distress or interfering with your ability to get along with others or do your job?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I was saying before, it's mostly just at work. Getting complaints and... having to be corrected because I missed parts of the instructions. It just causes friction with the other cleaners when we're doing the room turnovers or sorting out supplies. My supervisor wouldn't have pushed me toward that assistance program if it wasn't... you know, interfering. So... yeah. I guess it does.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced this restlessness and need to keep moving consistently throughout your adult life, or have there been periods where it was less of an issue?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't... I don't really sit around thinking about how I was years ago. I guess I've just always been this way. I couldn't tell you if there were times it was less of an issue, I just... I haven't really kept track of it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF90.0, F90.8, F90.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Um... I guess I'm here because I just... I have a hard time sitting still. \n\nIt's like I've got this motor humming under my skin whenever I'm supposed to just sit there. Like in staff meetings at work, or... church event briefings, things like that. I just end up shifting around in my chair, or standing up. Usually I just have to find some excuse to get up and move around. \n\nThat's... pretty much it.\n\nDoctor: Does this restlessness happen only in situations where you're expected to sit still, like meetings, or does it happen at other times too?\n\nPatient: Um... no, it's other times too, I suppose. \n\nWhen I'm on my shift... I just go from mopping to checking the trash, maybe straightening up chairs. I don't really stop. And... even when I get home, I don't usually sit down. I'll just go out to the garage and putter around, or... re-sort my tools. Just keep moving, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel impatient or irritated when you're unable to move around?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I haven't really... paid attention to that. I don't think I get mad or anything. I just... prefer to be doing something, I guess. I haven't really thought about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse, doing things without fully thinking them through?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so. I haven't really noticed anything like that. I just... go about my day, mostly. Do my job. I don't really make a lot of sudden decisions or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention to details or making careless mistakes in your work or daily activities?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My work is... pretty routine. I just do what I'm supposed to. I haven't had anyone tell me I'm missing things or... messing up, so I think I do fine with that.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice that you had a hard time sitting still?\n\nPatient: I guess... it goes back a long way. Even in grade school, I remember getting in trouble for popping out of my seat. I'd talk over the teacher or just rush ahead before they finished the directions. My mother... she used to say I acted like I was wound too tight, even back then. So, I guess it's always been there.\n\nDoctor: Does this restlessness and need to keep moving happen in all parts of your life, like at home, at work, and when you're with friends?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. On my shifts, I just... keep moving. I'll go right from mopping to checking the trash, straightening chairs... I don't really stop. And at home, it's mostly the same. I don't really just sit down after work. I'll go out and start puttering in the garage, maybe... re-sorting my tools or something. Just staying busy, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on a task that you lose track of time or forget about everything else around you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I just... I do my work and move on to the next thing. I don't really get stuck on one thing for hours like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to keep moving and restlessness causes problems for you in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: I guess it does, yeah. At work... well, there have been some complaints lately. I tend to miss parts of what they tell me to do, just rushing through, I suppose. It causes some friction with the others, especially when we're turning over rooms or trying to figure out supplies. It got to the point where my supervisor... he suggested I look into the employee assistance program. So, yeah, it's causing some issues.\n\nDoctor: Does your restlessness or need to keep moving ever cause problems for you in your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I mean... my wife says I can't sit still through a movie, but... I don't know if I'd call it a problem. We just... we don't talk about it much.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your need to keep moving is triggered by feeling anxious, stressed, or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I'm not really... I don't get anxious or stressed like that. I just... I just like to keep busy. It's just how I am, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your restlessness and need to keep moving are causing you a lot of distress or interfering with your ability to get along with others or do your job?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I was saying before, it's mostly just at work. Getting complaints and... having to be corrected because I missed parts of the instructions. It just causes friction with the other cleaners when we're doing the room turnovers or sorting out supplies. My supervisor wouldn't have pushed me toward that assistance program if it wasn't... you know, interfering. So... yeah. I guess it does.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced this restlessness and need to keep moving consistently throughout your adult life, or have there been periods where it was less of an issue?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't... I don't really sit around thinking about how I was years ago. I guess I've just always been this way. I couldn't tell you if there were times it was less of an issue, I just... I haven't really kept track of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty waiting for your turn or interrupt others during conversations?\n\nPatient: I guess so. I just... I don't like standing around. Like at work, if I'm waiting for the freight elevator, or... waiting on whoever has the supply closet key. Or if someone else is taking their time deciding which room we need to clean next. \n\nIt just... I get kind of antsy. Maybe a bit short with people. I'll start pacing, or... I guess I kind of push to get moving before it's really my turn. I just want to get on with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty sitting still and need to keep moving is more related to not being able to focus or pay attention, or is it more about feeling restless and having extra energy?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I never really thought about trying to separate the two. I just... I just feel like I need to move. I don't know if it's about focus or energy. It's just how I am.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I haven't really thought about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse, doing things without fully thinking them through?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so. I haven't really noticed anything like that. I just... go about my day, mostly. Do my job. I don't really make a lot of sudden decisions or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention to details or making careless mistakes in your work or daily activities?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My work is... pretty routine. I just do what I'm supposed to. I haven't had anyone tell me I'm missing things or... messing up, so I think I do fine with that.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice that you had a hard time sitting still?\n\nPatient: I guess... it goes back a long way. Even in grade school, I remember getting in trouble for popping out of my seat. I'd talk over the teacher or just rush ahead before they finished the directions. My mother... she used to say I acted like I was wound too tight, even back then. So, I guess it's always been there.\n\nDoctor: Does this restlessness and need to keep moving happen in all parts of your life, like at home, at work, and when you're with friends?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. On my shifts, I just... keep moving. I'll go right from mopping to checking the trash, straightening chairs... I don't really stop. And at home, it's mostly the same. I don't really just sit down after work. I'll go out and start puttering in the garage, maybe... re-sorting my tools or something. Just staying busy, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on a task that you lose track of time or forget about everything else around you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I just... I do my work and move on to the next thing. I don't really get stuck on one thing for hours like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to keep moving and restlessness causes problems for you in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: I guess it does, yeah. At work... well, there have been some complaints lately. I tend to miss parts of what they tell me to do, just rushing through, I suppose. It causes some friction with the others, especially when we're turning over rooms or trying to figure out supplies. It got to the point where my supervisor... he suggested I look into the employee assistance program. So, yeah, it's causing some issues.\n\nDoctor: Does your restlessness or need to keep moving ever cause problems for you in your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I mean... my wife says I can't sit still through a movie, but... I don't know if I'd call it a problem. We just... we don't talk about it much.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your need to keep moving is triggered by feeling anxious, stressed, or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I'm not really... I don't get anxious or stressed like that. I just... I just like to keep busy. It's just how I am, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your restlessness and need to keep moving are causing you a lot of distress or interfering with your ability to get along with others or do your job?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I was saying before, it's mostly just at work. Getting complaints and... having to be corrected because I missed parts of the instructions. It just causes friction with the other cleaners when we're doing the room turnovers or sorting out supplies. My supervisor wouldn't have pushed me toward that assistance program if it wasn't... you know, interfering. So... yeah. I guess it does.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced this restlessness and need to keep moving consistently throughout your adult life, or have there been periods where it was less of an issue?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't... I don't really sit around thinking about how I was years ago. I guess I've just always been this way. I couldn't tell you if there were times it was less of an issue, I just... I haven't really kept track of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty waiting for your turn or interrupt others during conversations?\n\nPatient: I guess so. I just... I don't like standing around. Like at work, if I'm waiting for the freight elevator, or... waiting on whoever has the supply closet key. Or if someone else is taking their time deciding which room we need to clean next. \n\nIt just... I get kind of antsy. Maybe a bit short with people. I'll start pacing, or... I guess I kind of push to get moving before it's really my turn. I just want to get on with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty sitting still and need to keep moving is more related to not being able to focus or pay attention, or is it more about feeling restless and having extra energy?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I never really thought about trying to separate the two. I just... I just feel like I need to move. I don't know if it's about focus or energy. It's just how I am.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told that you have attention deficit hyperactivity disorder or something similar?\n\nPatient: No. I mean, nobody's ever said anything like that to me. I've just always been a guy who likes to stay busy.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with staying focused on tasks or with controlling your physical restlessness and need to move?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I... I'm not really sure. I don't really analyze it like that. I just know I don't like sitting around doing nothing. I couldn't really tell you which one is a bigger issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following through on instructions or completing tasks because you lose focus or get distracted?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... I do my work. I get the job done. I don't really lose track of what I'm supposed to be doing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
